### [1] [Detecting and Explaining Fake News Short Videos with Multimodal Content and Real-World Evidence](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.12678)
*Yifeng Luo,Yupeng Li,Ming Tang,Jianxiong Guo,Liang Lan*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: The paper proposes NVKE-CEI, a unified system for fake news video detection and explanation (FNVDE). It addresses limitations of prior methods by introducing NVKE (news video keyframes extraction) for better frame selection based on visual and OCR-text similarity changes, and CEI (content and evidence information) framework that uses two LLM-based fact checkers fused by a lightweight judge model. The system outperforms state-of-the-art baselines.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Short-video platforms have become primary news sources, enabling widespread dissemination of fake news videos. Existing methods have two critical limitations: (1) frame selection strategies may omit veracity-relevant cues or provide insufficient temporal context, and (2) prior methods neglect either multimodal understanding or evidence retrieval.

Method: The proposed NVKE-CEI system consists of two components: (1) NVKE selects keyframes based on chronological changes in combined visual and OCR-text similarity; (2) CEI employs two specialized LLM-based fact checkers (content-based and evidence-based) whose outputs are fused by a lightweight judge model. The system generates content-grounded explanations.

Result: Extensive experiments show that NVKE-CEI outperforms state-of-the-art baselines while generating high-quality content-grounded explanations.

Conclusion: The proposed unified system effectively addresses the limitations of prior methods by combining improved keyframe extraction with multimodal fact-checking, achieving superior performance in fake news video detection and explanation.

Abstract: Short-video platforms have become a primary news source for the public, which has also enabled the widespread dissemination of fake news videos. We study the task of fake news video detection and explanation (FNVDE). Existing methods face two critical limitations. First, commonly used frame selection strategies may omit veracity-relevant cues or provide insufficient temporal context for understanding news videos. Second, prior methods neglect either multimodal understanding or evidence retrieval. To address these limitations, we propose NVKE-CEI, a unified system that integrates a news video keyframes extraction method (NVKE) and an FNVDE framework leveraging both content and evidence information (CEI). NVKE selects keyframes based on chronological changes in combined visual and OCR-text similarity. CEI employs two specialized LLM-based fact checkers (content-based and evidence-based) whose outputs are fused by a lightweight judge model. Extensive experiments show that NVKE-CEI outperforms state-of-the-art baselines while generating high-quality content-grounded explanations.

</details>


### [2] [UniMo: Unifying Human and Animal Motion Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.12342)
*Zeyu Zhang,Zhiyuan Zhang,Siheng Wang,Yiran Wang,Danning Li,Ian Reid,Richard Hartley*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: UniMo is a unified point cloud-based framework for 3D motion generation across humans and animals, addressing skeletal topology diversity and dataset limitations through parametric-to-unparametric representation conversion and dynamic sampling, accompanied by a large-scale dataset (UniML3D) with 145,907 motion sequences.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Extending text-driven human motion generation to animals faces two key challenges: (1) animals have diverse skeletal topologies unlike standard human structures, making unified modeling difficult and requiring inefficient per-species models; (2) existing animal motion datasets are limited in scale and annotation quality.

Method: UniMo converts parametric skeletons into unparametric point cloud representations to bypass topological discrepancies, enhanced by dynamic sampling that allocates more points to active joints. Additionally, UniML3D dataset was created with 145,907 motion sequences and 433,388 captions (102x larger than existing animal datasets).

Result: State-of-the-art results achieved on UniML3D and three public benchmarks: HumanML3D, KIT-ML, and AnimalML3D, demonstrating feasibility and effectiveness of unified human-animal motion generation.

Conclusion: The proposed approach successfully enables unified motion generation across humans and animals, addressing topological diversity through point cloud representation and significantly advancing the field through both methodological innovation and dataset contribution.

Abstract: The conditional generation of 3D motion has emerged as a key research topic due to its wide applicability across robotics, AR/VR, gaming, and content creation. However, extending recent advances in text-driven human motion generation to the animal domain remains challenging due to two core limitations. First, animals exhibit highly diverse skeletal topologies, unlike the standard human structure, making unified modeling across species difficult and leading to inefficient per-species models. Second, existing animal motion datasets suffer from limited scale and annotation quality, constraining model performance. To address these challenges, we propose UniMo, a unified point cloud-based motion generation framework that bypasses topological discrepancies by converting parametric skeletons into unparametric representations, further enhanced by dynamic sampling that allocates more points to active joints. Additionally, we present UniML3D, a large-scale motion-language dataset spanning both human and animal categories, containing 145,907 motion sequences and 433,388 captions-over 102x larger than existing animal datasets. Our method achieves state-of-the-art results on UniML3D and three public benchmarks including HumanML3D, KIT-ML, and AnimalML3D, demonstrating the feasibility and effectiveness of unified human-animal motion generation. Website: https://steve-zeyu-zhang.github.io/UniMo.

</details>


### [3] [UFO: Chain-of-Evaluation for Omni-Condition Alignment in Multi-Modal Image Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.12397)
*Danning Zhang,Yijing Lin,Shuhan Zhuang,Mengqi Huang,Shaojin Wu,Shancheng Fang,Zhendong Mao*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: UFO is the first unified framework for omni-condition alignment simultaneous evaluation in multi-modal image generation, addressing the limitation of existing methods that evaluate each modal condition in isolation rather than simultaneously.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing evaluation methods for multi-modal image generation assess alignment with each modal condition in isolation, which contradicts the simultaneous condition alignment objective of multi-modal image generation and leads to poor consistency with human judgments.

Method: UFO introduces a novel Atomized Chain-of-Evaluation paradigm that: (1) decomposes omni-condition alignment into a sequential chain of fine-grained, disentangled Atomic Evaluation Units (AEUs), (2) categorizes them into distinct modality-relevance classes, and (3) employs general or dedicated functional calls for accurate verification of different AEU types. The framework also includes UFO-Bench, a dedicated benchmark for evaluating customization models under diverse mutual interactions of textual and visual conditions.

Result: UFO achieves the highest correlation with human evaluation preferences, delivering an average improvement of 15.25% over existing methods.

Conclusion: UFO represents a significant advancement in multi-modal image generation evaluation by addressing the simultaneous condition alignment problem through its atomized chain-of-evaluation approach, providing more consistent evaluation that aligns with human judgments.

Abstract: Multi-modal image generation, particularly subject-driven customization, has garnered growing attention in recent years. Despite the rapid advancement of generative models, their evaluation remains largely lagging. Existing methods, whether embedding-based or Multi-modal Large Language Model (MLLM)-based, evaluate alignment with each modal condition in isolation, which contradicts the simultaneous condition alignment objective of multi-modal image generation, leading to poor consistency with human judgments. To address this challenge, we propose UFO, the first unified framework for omni-condition alignment simultaneous evaluation. Specifically, UFO introduces a novel Atomized Chain-of-Evaluation paradigm, \emph{i.e.}, it first decomposes omni-condition alignment into a sequential chain of fine-grained, disentangled Atomic Evaluation Units (AEUs), categorizes them into distinct modality-relevance classes, and then employs general or dedicated functional calls for accurate verification of different AEU types. Experimental results demonstrate that UFO achieves the highest correlation with human evaluation preferences, delivering an average improvement of 15.25\%. Furthermore, we present UFO-Bench, a dedicated benchmark designed to holistically evaluate the performance of existing customization models under the diverse mutual interactions of textual and visual conditions.

</details>


### [4] [PhysioAI: Clinical Knowledge-Guided Semantic Supervision for Skeleton-Based Physiotherapy Action Recognition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.12491)
*Jie Cao,Euijoon Ahn,Anwar Hassan,Jinman Kim*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: PhysioAI is a clinical knowledge-guided semantic supervision framework for skeleton-based physiotherapy exercise recognition. It injects structured physiotherapy knowledge into skeleton representation learning by using a Clinical Knowledge Dictionary (CKD) encoded via CLIP, providing class-specific semantic targets during training while requiring only skeleton inputs at inference.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing skeleton-based action recognition methods are designed for large-scale daily-action benchmarks rather than rehabilitation scenarios. Rehabilitation datasets are small with subtle kinematic differences between exercise classes, and participants with motor impairments exhibit increased intra-class variability, making reliable recognition more difficult.

Method: PhysioAI combines graph-based spatiotemporal modeling of human movement with training-time semantic anchors derived from a Clinical Knowledge Dictionary (CKD). The CKD descriptions are encoded using a frozen CLIP model and projected into an anchor space, providing class-specific semantic targets for skeleton representation learning. The CKD-derived anchors are used only during training; inference requires only skeleton inputs.

Result: Under subject-disjoint evaluation, PhysioAI achieves 99.03±1.34% on KiMoRe Overall, 94.64±7.36% on Hard-67 stress test, and 87.44±7.69% on UI-PRMD Overall. These results exceed the strongest comparator by 0.27, 2.87, and 1.33 percentage points respectively.

Conclusion: Structured clinical knowledge can serve as an effective source of training-time supervision for physiotherapy action recognition, demonstrating the value of integrating domain-specific knowledge into skeleton-based action recognition models for rehabilitation applications.

Abstract: Skeleton-based action recognition can support automated tracking of physiotherapy exercises, particularly in remote rehabilitation settings where continuous in-person supervision is impractical. However, most existing methods are developed for large-scale daily-action benchmarks rather than rehabilitation scenarios. Public rehabilitation exercise datasets are typically small, with only subtle kinematic differences between exercise classes. For participants with motor impairments, exercise execution may also deviate from standard movement patterns in amplitude, speed, and coordination, increasing intra-class variability and making reliable recognition more difficult for skeleton-based models. We propose PhysioAI, a clinical knowledge-guided semantic supervision framework that injects structured physiotherapy knowledge into skeleton representation learning. PhysioAI combines graph-based spatiotemporal modelling of human movement with training-time semantic anchors derived from a structured Clinical Knowledge Dictionary (CKD). The CKD descriptions are encoded using a frozen Contrastive Language-Image Pre-training (CLIP) model and projected into an anchor space, where they provide class-specific semantic targets for skeleton representation learning. The resulting CKD-derived anchors are used only during skeleton-model training; inference requires only skeleton inputs. Under subject-disjoint evaluation, PhysioAI achieves $99.03\pm1.34\%$ on KiMoRe Overall, $94.64\pm7.36\%$ on the Hard-67 stress test, and $87.44\pm7.69\%$ on UI-PRMD Overall. These results exceed the strongest comparator for each endpoint by $0.27$, $2.87$, and $1.33$ percentage points (pp), respectively. These findings demonstrate that structured clinical knowledge can serve as an effective source of training-time supervision for physiotherapy action recognition.

</details>


### [5] [RelateAnything: Real-Time Open-Vocabulary Relation Prediction From Any Inputs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.12552)
*Maëlic Neau*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: RelateAnything is a 53M-parameter model for open-vocabulary scene graph generation that takes an image and regions from any source, returning scored relations over a predicate vocabulary supplied at inference as strings. Object labels are never inputs, allowing the region source to change without retraining.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Scene-graph models remain tied to fixed predicate sets (50-56) due to three obstacles: (1) no relation corpus is both free-text and verified, (2) label-conditioned architectures cannot accept unseen vocabularies, and (3) standard metrics reward agreement with training corpus, causing larger vocabularies to score as regression. Open-vocabulary detection has moved taxonomy to input, but relation prediction has not.

Method: The model uses positive-unlabeled supervision with a text encoder that separates antonyms (contrastive encoders embed at cosine 0.95). The vocabulary is a bank of text embeddings rather than a learned classifier. Training required building RA-4M corpus (474k images, 4.3M relations over 10,102 free-text predicates generated against numbered box markers and geometrically verified). Evaluation uses OV-SGG-Bench with six axes across datasets.

Result: RelateAnything achieves 2.3-3.3x mean recall of the strongest open-vocabulary method of comparable scale, margins that survive a real detector, and leads a 3B-VLM scene-graph model on both metrics at under 2% of its parameters. It runs at 20 ms/frame. In-domain measurement overstates transfer gains approximately 5x.

Conclusion: The model, corpus (RA-4M), and benchmark (OV-SGG-Bench) are publicly available. The approach successfully enables arbitrary predicate vocabularies at inference time, decoupling relation prediction from object labels and fixed taxonomies.

Abstract: Open-vocabulary detection accepts any class list at inference, and promptable segmentation returns regions without class names: the taxonomy has left the model and become an input. Relation prediction has not. Scene-graph models are still trained and evaluated on the 50 or 56 predicates of one annotation style, their relation head conditioned on object labels and so tied to one detector. Three obstacles explain this, none primarily modelling: no relation corpus is both free-text and verified, a label-conditioned architecture cannot accept a vocabulary it was not trained on, and the standard metric rewards agreement with the training corpus, so a larger vocabulary scores as a regression. We present RelateAnything, a 53M-parameter model taking an image and regions from any source and returning scored relations over a predicate vocabulary supplied at inference as strings. Object labels are never an input, so the region source can change without retraining, and the vocabulary is a bank of text embeddings, not a learned classifier. It runs at 20 ms/frame. Training over 19,103 predicates requires positive-unlabeled supervision and a text encoder that separates antonyms, which contrastive encoders embed at cosine 0.95. To supply the supervision we build RA-4M, 474k images and 4.3M relations over 10,102 free-text predicates, generated against numbered box markers and geometrically verified. To measure it we build OV-SGG-Bench, six axes scored across datasets that the priors standard recall rewards cannot satisfy. On three cross-dataset benchmarks and a fourth zero-shot, RelateAnything has 2.3-3.5x the mean recall of the strongest open-vocabulary method of comparable scale, margins that survive a real detector, and leads a 3B-VLM scene-graph model on both metrics at under 2% of its parameters. In-domain measurement overstates transfer gains ~5x. Model, corpus and benchmark are public.

</details>


### [6] [KAD-Net: Kinematics-Aware Decoupled Learning for Robust 3D Hand Pose Estimation from a Single Depth Image](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.12559)
*Jun Lu,Zhenming Chen,Lin Chen,Kanlun Tan,Xiaoling Li,Qiao Liu*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: KAD-Net is a novel deep learning framework for 3D hand pose estimation from single depth images that addresses challenges of hand complexity and self-occlusion through a Finger Topology Constraint module and a task-decoupled hierarchical multitask architecture, achieving state-of-the-art performance on ICVL, NYU, and MSRA benchmarks.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing 3D hand pose estimation methods struggle with two key challenges: 1) complexity of hand kinematics and self-occlusion make it difficult to comprehensively model topological dependencies among hand joints, and 2) traditional hierarchical multitask architectures enforce shared feature spaces for 2D joint localization and depth estimation, causing mutual interference between tasks.

Method: The paper proposes two key innovations: 1) A Finger Topology Constraint (FTC) module that uses three consecutive finger joints to construct local kinematic representations, supplementing kinematic features of distal joints and leveraging structural context from visible joints to locate occluded distal joints; 2) A task-decoupled hierarchical multitask framework that separates 2D joint localization from depth estimation with dedicated multitask learning for depth regression, isolating UV and depth features to mitigate mutual interference and negative transfer.

Result: KAD-Net outperforms existing methods on several benchmark datasets (ICVL, NYU, and MSRA), achieving state-of-the-art accuracy in 3D hand pose estimation.

Conclusion: The proposed KAD-Net effectively addresses the challenges of hand kinematics complexity and self-occlusion through kinematics-aware constraints and task decoupling, demonstrating superior performance for 3D hand pose estimation with potential applications in human-computer interaction, virtual reality, and gesture-based control systems.

Abstract: Due to the complexity of hand kinematics and self-occlusion, existing 3D hand pose estimation methods based on single depth images struggle to comprehensively model the topological dependencies among hand joints. Furthermore, traditional hierarchical multitask architectures enforce a shared feature space for both 2D joint localization and depth estimation, which can induce mutual interference. To address these challenges, we propose a Kinematics-Aware Decoupled Learning Network (KAD-Net) for robust 3D hand pose estimation. Specifically, we first design a Finger Topology Constraint (FTC) module to enhance the representation of distal joints. This module utilizes three consecutive finger joints to construct a local kinematic representation to impose topological constraints, which supplements the kinematic features of the distal joints. The FTC module leverages the structural context from visible joints to assist in locating occluded distal joints, thereby improving robustness to occlusion. Additionally, we propose a task-decoupled hierarchical multitask framework. This framework separates 2D joint localization from depth estimation and incorporates a dedicated multitask learning strategy for depth regression, effectively isolating the UV and depth features to mitigate mutual interference and negative transfer. Extensive experiments demonstrate that KAD-Net outperforms existing methods on several benchmark datasets (ICVL, NYU, and MSRA), achieving state-of-the-art accuracy in 3D hand pose estimation. Potential applications of KAD-Net include human-computer interaction, virtual reality and gesture-based control systems.

</details>


### [7] [An Ultra-Widefield Swept-Source OCTA Dataset and a Polar-Gated Mamba Network for Retinal Vessel Segmentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.12574)
*Yang Liu,Yibing Shen,Keming Zhao,Cenk Jiang,Zhenghang Qian,Zhicheng Du,Chen Xiong,Qidong Shao,Zijun Lin,Yunqi Hu,Jingjing Zhou,Lian Zhang,Peter E. Lobie,Peiwu Qin,Chengming Yang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper introduces WOIVES, the first public benchmark dataset for ultra-widefield SS-OCTA vessel segmentation (206 eyes, 24×20mm² FOV), and PG-Mamba, a visual state space model with polar-coordinate scan orders and dynamic FOV gating. PG-Mamba achieved superior performance over seven competing methods in segmentation metrics and quantitative vascular analysis.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Ultra-widefield SS-OCTA enables large-area retinal vascular imaging, but lacks dedicated public benchmarks and comprehensive evaluation for quantitative vascular analysis at this scale.

Method: Dataset: WOIVES with 206 eyes from 152 participants spanning emmetropia to high myopia, with soft probability vessel annotations. Model: PG-Mamba, a visual state space model using two complementary polar-coordinate scan orders and an auxiliary Dynamic FOV Gating module for spatial modulation at the bottleneck.

Result: PG-Mamba outperformed seven competitive approaches on broad segmentation metrics under cross-validation. It achieved the lowest median absolute errors for vessel density, fractal dimension, and vessel length density.

Conclusion: PG-Mamba demonstrates superior performance in UWF SS-OCTA vessel segmentation, establishing a new benchmark with the WOIVES dataset for advancing quantitative vascular analysis in retinal imaging.

Abstract: Ultra-widefield (UWF) swept-source optical coherence tomography angiography (SS-OCTA) enables large-area retinal vascular imaging, yet vessel segmentation at this scale lacks dedicated public benchmarks and comprehensive evaluation for quantitative vascular analysis. We introduce WOIVES, to our knowledge the first publicly available UWF SS-OCTA vessel-segmentation dataset, comprising 206 eyes from 152 participants with a 24x20mm^2 field of view. WOIVES spans emmetropia to high myopia and provides soft probability vessel annotations. We further propose PG-Mamba, a visual state space model that enhances conventional directional scans with two complementary polar-coordinate scan orders. An auxiliary Dynamic FOV Gating module performs spatial modulation at the bottleneck. PG-Mamba outperformed seven competitive approaches on broad segmentation metrics under cross-validation. It achieved the lowest median absolute errors for vessel density, fractal dimension, and vessel length density. WOIVES is publicly available on Zenodo (DOI: 10.5281/zenodo.21904672), and the PG-Mamba code is available at https://github.com/syb1234567/PG-Mamba.

</details>


### [8] [RA-SOD: Reliability-Aware RGB-T Salient Object Detection under Modality Degradation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.12622)
*Hongbo Gao,Zhengyu Li,Xueru Nie,Dihao Zhu,Lijun Zhao,Yunke Wang,Chang Xu*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: RA-SOD is a reliability-aware RGB-T salient object detection framework that addresses modality degradation issues in RGB-thermal imaging by modeling modality reliability and integrating it into feature learning and cross-modal fusion through three novel mechanisms.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: RGB-T salient object detection suffers from unreliable modality cues due to environmental degradation: RGB images degrade under low illumination, motion blur, and noise, while thermal imagery suffers from contrast compression and sensor artifacts. These unreliable perceptual evidence mislead cross-modal fusion and deteriorate detection performance.

Method: The framework proposes three key components: (1) Reliability-conditioned representation that adaptively compensates degraded modality features while preserving structural cues; (2) Uncertainty-guided dual-stream refinement that progressively corrects cross-modal representations while suppressing unreliable evidence; (3) Pixel-wise modality competition mechanism that dynamically selects modality cues according to spatial reliability for fine-grained fusion.

Result: Extensive experiments on four benchmarks (VT821, VT1000, VT5000, and VT-IMAG) demonstrate that RA-SOD achieves state-of-the-art performance and exhibits strong robustness under severe modality degradation.

Conclusion: The proposed RA-SOD framework effectively addresses modality reliability issues in RGB-T salient object detection by explicitly modeling and integrating reliability information into the detection pipeline, achieving superior performance across multiple benchmarks.

Abstract: RGB-Thermal (RGB-T) salient object detection leverages complementary cues from visible and thermal modalities to improve robustness in challenging environments. However, in real-world scenarios, the reliability of each modality is inherently unstable: RGB images degrade under low illumination, motion blur, and noise, while thermal imagery often suffers from contrast compression and sensor artifacts. Such degradation introduces unreliable perceptual evidence that can mislead cross-modal fusion and significantly deteriorate detection performance. To address this challenge, we propose RA-SOD, a reliability-aware RGB-T salient object detection framework that explicitly models modality reliability and integrates it into feature learning and cross-modal fusion. First, we introduce a reliability-conditioned representation that adaptively compensates degraded modality features while preserving structural cues. Second, an uncertainty-guided dual-stream refinement strategy progressively corrects cross-modal representations while suppressing unreliable evidence. Finally, we propose a pixel-wise modality competition mechanism that dynamically selects modality cues according to spatial reliability for fine-grained fusion. Extensive experiments on four benchmarks (VT821, VT1000, VT5000, and VT-IMAG) demonstrate that RA-SOD achieves state-of-the-art performance and exhibits strong robustness under severe modality degradation. Code and models are available at https://github.com/zaoxienian/RA-SOD.

</details>


### [9] [Beyond Ambiguous Visual Cues: Studying Physiological Disruptions and Cross-Modal Inconsistencies in Deepfake Videos](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.12668)
*Chenxi Yang,Yassine Ouzar,Larbi Boubchir*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper proposes a bidirectional co-attention fusion detector that jointly models rPPG signals and facial behavior tokens to detect deepfakes. The approach achieves 92.80% AUC on face swapping and 96.78% AUC on motion transfer deepfakes, outperforming single-modality and feature-level fusion baselines. Transfer learning on Celeb-DF-v2 achieves 91.20% accuracy.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing deepfake detection benchmarks lack physiological ground truth, and current detectors underexplore the cross-level relationship between facial features and physiological dynamics, often relying on late fusion or rPPG features alone.

Method: 1) Construct high-fidelity deepfake manipulations on real rPPG datasets (COHFACE and UBFC-rPPG) 2) Propose a bidirectional co-attention fusion detector that jointly models rPPG and facial behavior tokens 3) The mechanism explicitly captures cross-level dependencies between pulse dynamics and facial motion to learn a robust joint authenticity representation 4) Subject-disjoint 5-fold evaluation

Result: Achieving 92.80% AUC on constructed datasets using face swapping and 96.78% AUC on motion transfer. The model outperforms both rPPG-only single modality baseline and best feature-level fusion methods. Transfer-learning result on Celeb-DF-v2 achieves 91.20% accuracy and 86.08% AUC.

Conclusion: The proposed bidirectional co-attention fusion detector effectively captures cross-level dependencies between physiological signals and facial behavior, achieving superior performance across different deepfake manipulation types. The transfer learning results suggest applicability under target-domain adaptation.

Abstract: Recent deepfake detection studies increasingly suggest remote photoplethysmography (rPPG) signals as an authenticity cue. However, existing benchmarks lack physiological ground truth, and current detectors underexplore the cross-level relationship between facial features and physiological dynamics, often relying on late fusion or rPPG features alone. In this paper, we construct high-fidelity deepfake manipulations on established real rPPG datasets (COHFACE and UBFC-rPPG) to investigate how forgeries disrupt natural physiological signals and facial behavior at the same time. Building on this analysis, we propose a bidirectional co-attention fusion detector that jointly models rPPG and facial behavior tokens. This mechanism explicitly captures the cross-level dependencies between pulse dynamics and facial motion to learn a robust, joint authenticity representation. Extensive experiments using a subject-disjoint 5-fold evaluation demonstrate the superiority of our approach. Achieving a 92.80\% AUC on constructed datasets using face swapping and 96.78\% AUC on motion transfer, our model outperforms both the rPPG-only single modality baseline and the best feature-level fusion methods. Furthermore, transfer-learning result of the fusion detector on Celeb-DF-v2 while keeping both feature extractors fixed achieves 91.20\% accuracy and 86.08\% AUC, which suggests applicability under target-domain adaptation.

</details>


### [10] [Semantically Aligned Gradient-Driven Context-Preserving Image Editing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.12691)
*Chiranjeev Chiranjeev,Muskan Dosi,Mayank Vatsa,Richa Singh*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: IABEdit addresses the training-time blind spot in instruction-guided image editing where generative editors lack semantic verification. It introduces a model-agnostic framework that embeds differentiable semantic verification using a frozen VLM to extract spatially-aware descriptors from ground-truth edits, with a trainable aligner to reproduce them from generated outputs. The residual gradient teaches the generator both what and where to edit without inference-time VLM cost.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Instruction-guided image editing models suffer from a fundamental training-time blind spot: they are never required to semantically verify whether their outputs actually satisfy the instruction. Supervision stops at reconstruction and input textual-level conditioning, leading to incomplete edits, spatial spillover, and poor localization.

Method: IABEdit uses a frozen vision-language model to extract spatially-aware descriptors from ground-truth edits. A trainable aligner then reproduces these descriptors from the generated output. The residual between the two becomes a gradient signal that teaches the generator what to edit and where, with no inference-time VLM cost. The framework is compatible with diverse backbones including U-Net (Stable Diffusion) and MMDiT (FLUX) without altering their inference pipelines.

Result: On MagicBrush: improves structural fidelity by +3.49 DINO-I over best diffusion baseline and +1.26 over best overall baseline while remaining competitive on instruction alignment. Achieves state-of-the-art instruction adherence on RealEdit and EMU Edit benchmarks. On D-LORD surveillance benchmark: surpasses proprietary Gemini agent by +5.13 DINO-P under heavy occlusion where preserving identity is hardest. Human and GPT-4o evaluations confirm perceptually precise, well-localized edits.

Conclusion: Gradient-aligned VLM distillation holds up under real-world-like surveillance and occlusion conditions. The approach successfully addresses the training-time blind spot by embedding differentiable semantic verification, enabling precise and well-localized edits without sacrificing inference efficiency.

Abstract: Instruction-guided image editing has a training-time blind spot. Generative editors are never required to semantically verify whether their outputs actually satisfy the instruction. Supervision stops at reconstruction and input textual-level conditioning. This produces incomplete edits, spatial spillover, and poor localization. We present IABEdit, a model-agnostic framework that embeds differentiable semantic verification into training. A frozen vision-language model extracts spatially-aware descriptors from the ground-truth edit. A trainable aligner then reproduces them from the generated output. The residual between the two becomes a gradient that teaches the generator both what to edit and where, with no inference-time VLM cost. IABEdit is compatible with diverse backbones, including U-Net (Stable Diffusion) and MMDiT (FLUX), without altering their inference pipelines. On MagicBrush, it improves structural fidelity by +3.49 DINO-I over the best diffusion baseline and +1.26 over the best overall baseline, while remaining competitive on instruction alignment. It also achieves state-of-the-art instruction adherence performance on RealEdit and EMU Edit benchmarks based on embedding-based metrics. Most consequentially, on the D-LORD surveillance benchmark, it surpasses the proprietary Gemini agent by +5.13 DINO-P under heavy occlusion, where preserving identity is hardest. This shows that gradient-aligned VLM distillation holds up under real-world-like surveillance and occlusion conditions. Human and GPT-4o evaluations confirm perceptually precise, well-localized edits.

</details>


### [11] [AquaCubeAI-Powered Monitoring Turbidity on-board Φsat-2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.12744)
*Pietro Di Stasio,Francesca Razzano,Elisa Liparulo,Gabriele Meoni,Nicolas Longépé,Deodato Tapete,Paolo Gamba,Gilda Schirinzi,Silvia Liberata Ullo*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: AquaCubeAI is a lightweight machine learning system for onboard estimation of coastal water turbidity from satellite imagery, designed to reduce latency by performing inference directly on the satellite rather than relying on ground processing.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Conventional satellite workflows for coastal water quality monitoring introduce latency through downlink and ground processing, limiting responsiveness to rapidly evolving turbidity events. There is a need for lower-latency, more responsive monitoring capabilities.

Method: The approach uses a compact Multi-Layer Perceptron (MLP) trained on simulated Φsat-2 multispectral imagery paired with Copernicus Marine Service (CMEMS) High-Resolution Ocean Color turbidity products. A spatial block splitting protocol is employed to mitigate data leakage between training and evaluation sets. The model is deployed on an Intel Myriad Vision Processing Unit (VPU) for embedded inference.

Result: The system enables turbidity mapping and threshold-based anomaly detection with low-latency inference capabilities on low-power hardware, covering four European marine macro-regions.

Conclusion: AquaCubeAI demonstrates the feasibility of onboard, low-power machine learning inference for coastal water turbidity monitoring, enabling faster response to turbidity events through satellite-based processing rather than ground-based analysis.

Abstract: Timely monitoring of coastal water quality is critical for environmental protection, yet conventional satellite workflows rely on downlink and ground processing, introducing latency that can limit responsiveness to rapidly evolving turbidity events. To address this limitation, we propose AquaCubeAI, a lightweight machine-learning approach for onboard estimation of coastal water turbidity from Φsat-2 multispectral imagery. By shifting inference from the ground segment to the satellite, AquaCubeAI aims to enable lower-latency, more responsive, and more operationally useful turbidity monitoring under the strict compute and bandwidth constraints of spaceborne platforms. The model is trained on simulated Φsat-2 acquisitions spatially aligned with Copernicus Marine Service (CMEMS) High-Resolution Ocean Color (HR-OC) turbidity products over selected localized coastal sites spanning four European marine macro-regions. To provide a realistic evaluation of generalization in the presence of spatial correlation, we adopt a spatial block splitting protocol that mitigates data leakage between training and evaluation subsets. The main contributions of this work are: (i) a scalable dataset generation pipeline pairing simulated Φsat-2 multispectral patches with CMEMS HR-OC turbidity labels across selected localized European coastal sites; (ii) a compact Multi-Layer Perceptron (MLP)-based turbidity regressor trained under a leakage-aware geospatial split and tailored to embedded constraints; and (iii) a reformulation for dense spatial prediction via parameter sharing, enabling turbidity mapping and simple threshold-based anomaly masks for onboard decision logic. Embedded deployment on an Intel Myriad Vision Processing Unit (VPU) further confirms the feasibility of low-power hardware and supports low-latency inference from multispectral inputs.

</details>


### [12] [Same Encoder, Different Winner: A Paired-View Framework for Cell Painting Encoder Evaluation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.12761)
*Tim Treis,Nikita Moshkov,Johan Fredin Haslum,Shantanu Singh,Fabian J. Theis*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: The paper introduces CP-BG-Bench, a paired-view evaluation framework for Cell Painting vision encoders that tests the same central cell across four matched views (raw crop, segmented, and density-augmented variants). Testing three encoders on three datasets under four protocols reveals that single-metric ranking is insufficient, as different protocols rank encoders systematically differently along three interpretable axes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Cell Painting encoders are typically ranked by a single metric (replicate mAP), but this fails to capture how different methods handle cell versus background information, morphology versus context, and within-study versus across-batch generalization. The authors sought to understand why different evaluation protocols produce conflicting rankings.

Method: The authors created CP-BG-Bench, a paired-view framework that holds the central cell fixed across four matched views (C, S, CD, SD) and tests three encoders (DINOv3 ViT-B/16, OpenPhenom, SubCell) on three datasets (JUMP-CP, RxRx1, RxRx3-core) using four community-standard protocols (replicate mAP, scIB batch integration, CellProfiler feature prediction, cross-batch perturbation recall).

Result: The four protocols rank encoders systematically differently. Key findings: SubCell with segmented inputs retains 94% of crop replicate mAP but only 32% of crop R@10 on RxRx3-core. Density augmentation recovers 84% of the within-study C-to-S gap but only 8% of the cross-batch gap. Segmented views predict CellProfiler features as well as or better than crops on two of three datasets, inverting replicate-mAP rankings. The C-to-S gap varies by an order of magnitude across datasets but remains similar across encoders.

Conclusion: Single-metric ranking of Cell Painting encoders is protocol-sensitive. Protocol disagreements are interpretable as projections onto three axes: cell versus background, morphology versus context, and within-study versus across-batch. Background-driven gain is determined by experimental design rather than encoder choice. The authors will release paired-view datasets, reconstruction pipelines, 36 trained checkpoints, aggregated embeddings, and the evaluation suite.

Abstract: Vision encoders for Cell Painting are typically ranked by a single evaluation, commonly replicate mean average precision (mAP). We introduce CP-BG-Bench, a paired-view evaluation framework that holds the central cell fixed across four matched views (raw crop C, segmented S, and density-augmented variants CD and SD), ablating or augmenting surrounding pixels as a controlled intervention. Instantiating the framework on three datasets (JUMP-CP, RxRx1, RxRx3-core) and three encoders (DINOv3 ViT-B/16, OpenPhenom, SubCell) under four community-standard protocols (replicate mAP, scIB batch integration, CellProfiler feature prediction, cross-batch perturbation recall), we find that the four protocols rank the same encoders systematically differently, with disagreements decomposing along three axes: cell versus background, morphology versus context, and within-study versus across-batch. The largest effect: on RxRx3-core, SubCell with segmented inputs retains 94% of crop replicate mAP but only 32% of crop R@10, so the within-study signal preserved under segmentation is largely non-transferable; density augmentation recovers 84% of the within-study C-to-S gap but only 8% of the cross-batch gap. Segmented views predict CellProfiler features as well as or better than crops on two of three datasets, inverting the replicate-mAP ranking, and the C-to-S gap varies by an order of magnitude across datasets while remaining similar across encoders, indicating that background-driven gain is set by experimental design rather than by the encoder. Single-metric ranking of Cell Painting encoders is therefore sensitive to the protocol used, and protocol disagreements are interpretable as projections onto the three axes the paired-view design exposes. We will release the paired-view datasets, reconstruction pipelines, 36 trained checkpoints, aggregated embeddings, and the full evaluation suite.

</details>


### [13] [LG-PF: Lightweight Confidence-Guided Polarization Image Fusion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.12787)
*Zhuangfan Huang,Zhenyu Kuang,Gao Wang,Yang Liu,Haishu Tan,Xiaosong Li*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: LG-PF is a lightweight confidence-guided polarization image fusion framework that selectively transfers polarization details from DoLP images while preserving structural integrity from S0 images. It uses a Polarization Confidence Prior to estimate reliable polarization responses, a Mask-guided Multi-scale Fusion module for multi-scale feature regulation, and a Lightweight Context-aware Bounded Correction Head for stabilizing photometric transitions. The method achieves state-of-the-art results on MSP dataset with only 0.2936M parameters and 21.712ms inference time.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Polarization image fusion combines S0 (stable luminance/structure) with DoLP (material-sensitive details), but DoLP reliability varies spatially. Indiscriminate polarization transfer can amplify unstable responses or disrupt structural appearance anchored by S0.

Method: 1) Polarization Confidence Prior: estimates spatially reliable polarization responses
2) Mask-guided Multi-scale Fusion module: regulates polarization transfer across three feature scales
3) Lightweight Context-aware Bounded Correction Head: stabilizes local photometric and structural transitions
4) Confidence-guided optimization objectives: preserve reliable polarization details while suppressing unsupported responses

Result: Best results across all six evaluated metrics on MSP dataset; promising transferability on PIF and GAND datasets without fine-tuning. Only 0.2936M parameters and 21.712ms inference time per image.

Conclusion: LG-PF achieves competitive fusion quality with low computational cost through selective residual transfer guided by polarization confidence. The framework successfully balances preserving reliable polarization details while maintaining structural integrity from the intensity image.

Abstract: Polarization image fusion combines the stable luminance and structural information of the total- intensity image S0 with the material-sensitive details of the degree of linear polarization (DoLP) image. However, the reliability of DoLP varies spatially, and indiscriminate polarization transfer may amplify unstable responses or disturb the structural appearance anchored by S0. We therefore propose LG-PF, a lightweight confidence-guided framework that formulates polarization fusion as a selective residual transfer process. A Polarization Confidence Prior estimates spatially reliable polarization responses, a Mask-guided Multi-scale Fusion module regulates their transfer across three feature scales, and a Lightweight Context-aware Bounded Correction Head stabilizes local photometric and structural transitions. Confidence guidance is also incorporated into the optimization objectives to preserve reliable polarization details while suppressing unsupported responses. We also construct MSP, a multi-scene polarization fusion dataset containing 1000 pixel-aligned image pairs from 17 indoor and outdoor scene categories. LG-PF achieves the best results across all six evaluated metrics on MSP, while subset-based evaluations on PIF and GAND show promising transferability without fine-tuning. With only 0.2936 M parameters and an inference time of 21.712 ms per image, LG-PF achieves competitive fusion quality with low computational cost. The source code, dataset, and official data splits will be made publicly available upon publication.

</details>


### [14] [LGFN: Lightweight Gated RGB-Polarization Fusion with Modality-Availability Conditioning for Camouflaged Object Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.12798)
*Zhuangfan Huang,Xiaosong Li,Yang Liu,Tao Ye,Haishu Tan*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: LGFN is a lightweight gated RGB-polarization fusion framework for camouflaged object detection that supports both RGB-only and polarization-assisted configurations, selectable via a deterministic Modality Router. The multimodal configuration uses a Modality Gate, Gated Polarization Hub, and RGB-Polarization Cross Fusion to coordinate DoLP and AoP representations with RGB features.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Camouflaged object detection remains challenging when targets closely resemble surroundings. Existing methods typically assume fixed multimodal input configurations and entangle intra-polarization coordination with RGB-polarization interaction. A flexible framework that can adapt to polarization availability while effectively fusing RGB and polarization cues was needed.

Method: The proposed LGFN framework includes: (1) A deterministic Modality Router that selects configuration based on polarization availability; (2) An availability-conditioned Modality Gate that calibrates available polarization branches; (3) A Gated Polarization Hub that coordinates learned Degree of Linear Polarization (DoLP) and Angle of Polarization (AoP) representations with explicit polarization cues; (4) RGB-Polarization Cross Fusion that introduces coordinated representation into RGB hierarchy through controlled residual interaction. The method requires no sample-dependent statistics or handcrafted quality descriptors during inference.

Result: On the 230-image PCOD_1200 test set: RGB-only configuration achieves MAE=0.0090, Dice=0.8806, IoU=0.8144, outperforming all RGB-based methods on all six metrics. Under multimodal configuration, it outperforms PolarNet and IPNet on all six metrics. Compared to IPNet, it reduces parameters by 53.1%, FLOPs by 73.6%, and latency by 63.0%.

Conclusion: LGFN effectively addresses camouflaged object detection through a flexible RGB-polarization fusion framework. The RGB-only configuration achieves state-of-the-art results among RGB-based methods, while the multimodal configuration provides superior performance with significant efficiency gains over prior polarization-based methods.

Abstract: Camouflaged object detection (COD) is an important engineering task in intelligent optical perception, but it remains challenging when targets closely resemble their surroundings. Polarization imaging provides complementary physical cues, whereas existing methods typically assume fixed multimodal input configurations and entangle intra-polarization coordination with interaction between red-green-blue (RGB) and polarization representations. We propose LGFN, a lightweight gated RGB-polarization fusion framework supporting separately optimized RGB-only and polarization-assisted configurations. A deterministic Modality Router selects the appropriate configuration according to polarization availability. In the multimodal configuration, an availability-conditioned Modality Gate calibrates the available polarization branches; the Gated Polarization Hub coordinates learned degree of linear polarization (DoLP) and angle of polarization (AoP) representations with explicit polarization cues; and RGB-Polarization Cross Fusion introduces the coordinated representation into the RGB hierarchy through controlled residual interaction. The multimodal configuration requires neither sample-dependent statistics nor handcrafted quality descriptors during inference. On the complete 230-image PCOD_1200 test set, the RGB-only configuration achieves a mean absolute error of 0.0090, a Dice score of 0.8806, and an intersection over union of 0.8144, obtaining the best results on all six metrics among the evaluated RGB-based methods. Under a common local reevaluation protocol, the multimodal configuration outperforms PolarNet and IPNet on all six metrics. Relative to IPNet, it reduces the parameter count, floating-point operations, and latency by 53.1%, 73.6%, and 63.0%, respectively.

</details>


### [15] [Online Video Agent Harness for Long Video Understanding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.12818)
*Sen Yang,Boqiang Duan,Jing Yang,Weihao Bo,Jie Liu,Boyuan Tong,Ze Feng,Wenkang Zhang,Jingdong Wang,Hua Wu*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: VideoXAgent is a purely online video-agent harness for long video understanding that uses on-demand expert tool invocation (detection, OCR, ASR, face recognition, etc.) to progressively gather evidence, achieving competitive performance with frontier LMMs while using only ~50k tokens of context (15% of dense-packing baseline).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Long video understanding suffers from sparse query-relevant evidence distribution and "context rot" when packing dense frames into VLM context. Existing approaches rely on query-agnostic preprocessing or ad hoc tools, missing query-specific details and wasting computation.

Method: VideoXAgent starts from video file and user query, plans and decomposes tasks, invokes specialized expert tools on-demand (scripts, VLMs, domain models), and aggregates multimodal evidence while resolving conflicts. The system uses a data-driven taxonomy of atomic capabilities, objective evidence prompting, and budget-aware control to curb hallucination and non-termination.

Result: Across Video-MME-Long, LongVideoBench-Long, LVBench, and MINERVA, VideoXAgent achieves competitive performance with frontier LMMs and video agents under ~50k tokens context per sample. On MINERVA, it matches performance while using only 15% of the context of a 1,024-frame dense-packing baseline.

Conclusion: Strong long-video understanding can emerge from progressive agentic evidence seeking rather than packing the full video into a single context. The harness remains effective with a visually weak or even text-only orchestrator.

Abstract: Long video understanding often behaves like a visual needle-in-a-haystack problem: query-relevant evidence is sparsely distributed across long temporal spans, while packing dense frames into a single VLM context incurs \textit{context rot} and high cost. Existing video agents often rely on query-agnostic offline preprocessing or ad hoc tool sets, which can miss query-specific details and waste computation. In this work, we present VideoXAgent, a purely online video-agent harness for long video understanding that starts from the given video file and user query, plans and decomposes the task, invokes specialized expert tools on demand, and aggregates multimodal evidence to produce a final answer while resolving conflicts among observations. To support this on-demand invocation, we design a suite of heterogeneous expert tools guided by a data-driven taxonomy of atomic capabilities, spanning scripts, VLMs, and domain models (e.g., detection, OCR, ASR, face recognition). The harness further enforces objective evidence prompting and budget-aware control to curb hallucination and non-termination. Across Video-MME-Long, LongVideoBench-Long, LVBench, and MINERVA, VideoXAgent is competitive with frontier LMMs and video agents under a smaller context footprint---about 50k tokens of agent context per sample, even on hour-long videos. In particular, on complex video-reasoning benchmarks such as MINERVA, it matches this level while using only about 15\% of the context of a 1,024-frame dense-packing baseline. Notably, the harness remains effective with a visually weak or even text-only orchestrator, suggesting that strong long-video understanding can emerge from progressive agentic evidence seeking rather than from packing the full video into a single context. Project page: https://go-agent-x.github.io/video_agent_harness/

</details>


### [16] [SCDM: Spatial-Contextual Disentanglement Mamba via Differential Inference for Efficient Image Classification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.12825)
*Mustafa Bora Çelik,Hayriye Aktaş Dinçer,Ayse Keles*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: SCDM is a novel asymmetric dual-branch State Space Model for medical image analysis that disentangles disease-specific features from normal anatomical context through competitive feature learning, achieving 0.858 AUC with only 29.4M parameters and 1.44G FLOPs on RSNA Pneumonia dataset.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing SSM architectures (like VMamba) struggle to distinguish subtle pathological features from visually similar anatomical backgrounds due to entangled representations lacking explicit mechanisms to separate disease signals from normal anatomy.

Method: SCDM introduces an asymmetric dual-branch architecture with: (1) Positive Branch for extracting discriminative features, (2) Negative Branch that actively models and suppresses normal anatomical context, (3) Similarity-driven repulsion gate, and (4) Differential inference rule - all without requiring additional branch labels or increasing model capacity.

Result: On RSNA Pneumonia dataset: AUC of 0.858, 29.4M parameters, 1.44G FLOPs - significantly fewer than standard VMamba and vision transformer baselines while achieving competitive classification performance.

Conclusion: The differential mechanism yields highly precise localization, effectively isolating lesions by inhibiting irrelevant anatomical distractors. SCDM demonstrates that selective representational disentanglement can be achieved through competitive dual-branch learning without additional supervision or increased model capacity.

Abstract: State Space Models (SSMs), particularly VMamba, have emerged as efficient alternatives for modeling long-range dependencies in medical image analysis. However, distinguishing subtle pathological features from visually similar anatomical backgrounds remains a significant challenge. Existing SSM architectures often learn entangled representations, lacking explicit mechanisms to separate disease-specific signals from normal anatomy. To address this limitation, we propose Spatial-Contextual Differential Mamba (SCDM), an asymmetric dual-branch architecture designed for selective representational disentanglement. SCDM introduces a Positive Branch for extracting discriminative features and a Negative Branch that actively models and suppresses normal anatomical context. This separation is achieved through a similarity-driven repulsion gate and a differential inference rule, which promote competitive feature learning without requiring additional branch labels or increasing model capacity. Evaluated on the RSNA Pneumonia dataset, SCDM achieves competitive classification performance (AUC of 0.858) while requiring significantly fewer parameters (29.4M) and FLOPs (1.44G) compared to standard VMamba and vision transformer baselines. Furthermore, activation analyses demonstrate that our differential mechanism yields highly precise localization, effectively isolating lesions by inhibiting irrelevant anatomical distractors.

</details>


### [17] [CoralscapesV2: Panoptic and Fine-Grained Visual Scene Understanding in Coral Reefs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.12826)
*Jonathan Sauder,Thomas Ruckli,Gabrielė Strodomskytė,Ibrahim Souleiman Abdallah,Rahma Hassan Abdi,Djama Goumaneh Awaleh,Mohamed Houssein Farah,Moustapha Nour,Osama Sharhubil Saad,Mustafa Mohammed Khalafallah Altaib,Maysoon Kteifan,Farah Alsoqi,Eyad Zgool,Jafar Al-Omari,Temesgen Gebremeskel Gebreluel,Zekaria Zekeria Abdulkerim,Meron Ghirmay,Teklehaimanot Beraki,Devis Tuia,Guilhem Banc-Prandi*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: CoralscapesV2 is a new dataset for panoptic segmentation in coral reefs, extending the original Coralscapes dataset from 39 to 95 fine-grained visual categories, with 65k exhaustive fish instance mask annotations. It is the first dataset for panoptic segmentation in coral reefs, providing a challenging benchmark for contemporary semantic and instance segmentation models.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The global decline of coral reefs necessitates scaled-up ecological monitoring. Traditional standardized survey images are being replaced by unconstrained imagery on scalable platforms, requiring machine learning methods for fine-grained reef understanding from general-purpose reef imagery.

Method: The dataset increases size, scope, label completeness and quality for semantic segmentation. Fish annotations were meticulously completed by using video, revealing that annotation based only on static images is insufficient.

Result: CoralscapesV2 contains 95 fine-grained visual categories (up from 39) and 65k exhaustive fish instance mask annotations. It provides the first panoptic segmentation dataset for coral reefs, capturing a wide range of wild scenarios.

Conclusion: CoralscapesV2 represents an important step toward general-purpose panoptic segmentation in coral reefs, with substantial implications for scaling up coral reef monitoring. It can be applied to benthic cover mapping from robot or handheld videos, and automated quantification and understanding of fish behavior and fish-reef interactions.

Abstract: In order to design conservation and restoration strategies to counter the global decline of coral reefs, ecological monitoring of reefs needs to be scaled up dramatically. Computer vision methods are increasingly used to tackle the vast amount of data: as the paradigm of data collection in reefs shifts from highly standardized and constrained survey images to unconstrained imagery on scalable platforms, it is necessary to design machine learning methods that help to get a fine-grained understanding of reefs from general-purpose reef imagery. This paper provides CoralscapesV2, an extension of the Coralscapes dataset for general-purpose visual scene understanding in reefs. CoralscapesV2 increases the dataset size, scope, label completeness and quality for semantic segmentation, and extends the number of classes from 39 to 95 fine-grained visual categories. Furthermore, CoralscapesV2 provides 65k exhaustive fish instance mask annotations, meticulously annotated to completeness by using the video, revealing that annotation of fish based on only static images is insufficient. CoralscapesV2 is the first dataset for panoptic segmentation in coral reefs, capturing a wide range of scenarios in the wild, posing a challenging benchmark for contemporary semantic segmentation and instance segmentation models. CoralscapesV2 is an important step towards general-purpose panoptic segmentation in coral reefs, which has substantial implications for scaling up coral reef monitoring, as it can be employed in a wide range of applications from benthic cover mapping from robot or handheld videos to designing methods for automated quantification and understanding of fish behavior and fish-reef interactions.

</details>


### [18] [A Dual Cross-Attention Framework for Colposcopic CIN Grading and Swede Score Prediction Using a New Multi-Center Dataset](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.12827)
*Dania Khan,Nuzhat Aisha Shaikh,Asfina Hassan Juicy,Raiyun Kabir,S M Shahida,Taufiq Hasan*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A novel deep learning framework for automated cervical intraepithelial neoplasia (CIN) grading and Swede score prediction using dual-stream cross-attention architecture on a new multi-center colposcopy dataset, achieving 71.85% accuracy and 86.23% AUC-ROC for three-class CIN grading.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Cervical cancer burden disproportionately affects low- and middle-income countries (LMICs) due to shortage of trained specialists and subjective colposcopy-based screening. There is a need for AI-assisted tools to support risk-based triage in resource-limited settings.

Method: Dual-stream cross-attention architecture that fuses paired multimodal cervigrams to simulate expert visual reasoning. Custom composite loss function addresses severe class imbalances and scoring inconsistencies across five Swede score components. Dataset: BUET Multi-Center Colposcopy Dataset (publicly available).

Result: Three-class CIN grading: 71.85% accuracy, 86.23% AUC-ROC. Swede score component prediction: AUC-ROC ranging from 75.7% to 88.4%. Composite loss function yielded consistent F1-score improvements. Total Swede Score (0-10): MAE of 1.489.

Conclusion: The proposed deep learning framework outperforms existing methods and demonstrates potential for developing AI-assisted colposcopy screening tools to support risk-based triage in resource-limited healthcare settings.

Abstract: Cervical cancer is a major global health challenge, with disease burden falling disproportionately on low- and middle-income countries (LMICs) due to a shortage of trained specialists and the subjective nature of colposcopy-based screening. To address this challenge, we propose a novel deep learning framework for the automated grading of Cervical Intraepithelial Neoplasia (CIN) and the prediction of clinical Swede scores. We also introduce the BUET Multi-Center Colposcopy Dataset, a novel, multi-center cohort designed and annotated for Swede score prediction and CIN grading. Our proposed dual-stream cross-attention architecture mimics the visual reasoning of an expert colposcopist by explicitly fusing paired multimodal cervigrams to evaluate comparative tissue responses. Furthermore, we introduce a custom composite loss function to address severe class imbalances and scoring inconsistencies across the five Swede score components. The proposed framework achieved 71.85% accuracy and an 86.23% AUC-ROC for three-class CIN grading, outperforming existing methods. For Swede score component prediction, the architecture achieved AUC-ROC values ranging from 75.7% to 88.4%, with the composite loss function yielding consistent F1-score improvements. Finally, the total predicted Swede Score, which ranges between 0 and 10, shows a Mean Absolute Error (MAE) of 1.489. The results show that the proposed method can pave the way towards developing AI-assisted colposcopy screening tools to support risk-based triage in resource-limited healthcare settings. The dataset and source code are publicly available(url: https://github.com/mHealthBuet/BUET-colposcopy)

</details>


### [19] [Balancing Emotional Alignment and Semantic Consistency in Image Generation via Reinforcement Learning with Valence-Arousal Anchoring](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.12830)
*Jisheng Dang,Zhenxuan Wang,Bin Li,Ronghao Lin,Bin Hu,Tat-Seng Chua*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper proposes a flow-matching image-generation framework with continuous valence-arousal (VA) conditioning, Group Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO), and a neutral semantic anchor to address emotion-semantic drift in continuous emotion control for text-to-image generation. The method converts probability-flow ODE to marginal-preserving SDE and uses a CLIP-based VA regressor for reward signal, achieving better emotional alignment and semantic consistency than baselines.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing supervised emotion-injection methods for text-to-image generation suffer from emotion-semantic drift, where stronger emotional conditioning leads to unintended content changes (objects, layout, scene). The authors aim to improve affective alignment while preserving semantic content.

Method: 1) Flow-matching framework with continuous VA conditioning
2) Convert deterministic probability-flow ODE to marginal-preserving SDE for non-degenerate transition densities
3) Use frozen CLIP-based VA regressor for terminal reward (distance between predicted and target VA coordinates)
4) Use zero-VA conditioned image as feature-space reference for semantic preservation
5) Reduced denoising schedule for online RL sampling; original schedule at inference
6) Apply Group Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO) for training

Result: Experiments on 3,300 prompt-emotion combinations show substantially lower valence and arousal errors than VA-conditioned baseline, improved CLIPScore relative to EmotiCrafter, with a measurable trade-off in reference-free image quality.

Conclusion: Anchor-regularized Flow-GRPO is a practical approach to balancing emotional alignment and semantic consistency in continuous-affect image synthesis. The method successfully reduces emotion-semantic drift while maintaining affective control.

Abstract: Continuous emotion control in text-to-image generation requires a model to improve affective alignment without changing the objects, layout, or scene described by the prompt. Existing supervised emotion-injection methods often optimize feature-space proxies and may therefore exhibit emotion-semantic drift, in which stronger emotional conditioning is accompanied by unintended content changes. We address this problem with a flow-matching image-generation framework that combines continuous valence-arousal (VA) conditioning, Group Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO), and a neutral semantic anchor. The deterministic probability-flow ODE is converted into a marginal-preserving SDE, yielding non-degenerate transition densities for trajectory sampling and policy-ratio estimation. A frozen CLIP-based VA regressor supplies a terminal reward measuring the distance between the predicted and target VA coordinates, while an image generated from the same prompt under zero VA conditioning provides a feature-space reference for semantic preservation. A reduced denoising schedule is used for online RL sampling, whereas the original schedule is retained at inference. Experiments on 3,300 prompt-emotion combinations show substantially lower valence and arousal errors than the VA-conditioned baseline and an improved CLIPScore relative to EmotiCrafter, with a measurable trade-off in reference-free image quality. The results support anchor-regularized Flow-GRPO as a practical approach to balancing emotional alignment and semantic consistency in continuous-affect image synthesis.

</details>


### [20] [Self-supervised Pre-training Helps Retinal Disease Progression Modelling Most When Data Is Scarce](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.12834)
*Ifeoma Veronica Nwabufo,Julius Gervelmeyer,Sarah Müller,Philipp Berens*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Self-supervised pre-training on cross-sectional data enables building disease progression models when longitudinal data is scarce. A frozen encoder with lightweight survival head achieves clinically reasonable discrimination from a few hundred labeled samples, outperforming models trained from scratch in low-data regimes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Longitudinal imaging cohorts for disease progression modeling are scarce and small, while cross-sectional data is abundant. This study investigates which self-supervised pre-training strategy best supports progression modeling, particularly with limited labeled longitudinal data.

Method: Pre-trained encoders on large cross-sectional NAKO cohort and predicted time to late AMD on longitudinal AREDS dataset. Compared in-house self-supervised encoders against general-purpose (DINOv2) and domain-specific (RETFound) foundation models, across contrastive, masked-autoencoding, and self-distillation objectives, under frozen and fine-tuned protocols, across labeled training sets from 100 to 32,250 examples.

Result: Best model depends on usage: frozen encoder with few labels (typical longitudinal cohort regime) achieves clinically reasonable discrimination from a few hundred labeled samples, while models trained from scratch fail; this advantage fades under fine-tuning. Transfer is governed by self-supervision objective rather than corpus scale or domain match - an encoder pre-trained on modest cross-sectional cohort matches or exceeds larger in-domain foundation model.

Conclusion: Practical recipe for building progression models where longitudinal data is scarce: use a frozen self-supervised encoder with a lightweight survival head. The self-supervision objective matters more than corpus scale or domain match for transfer learning.

Abstract: Modelling how a disease progresses over time requires longitudinal imaging cohorts, which are scarce and small, whereas cross-sectional data -- one image per participant -- is abundant. Self-supervised pre-training on such data offers a way to bridge this gap, but it is unclear which strategy best supports progression modelling, or how that answer depends on the amount of labelled longitudinal data. We study this for age-related macular degeneration (AMD), pre-training encoders on the large cross-sectional NAKO cohort and predicting time to late AMD on the longitudinal AREDS dataset. We compare in-house self-supervised encoders against a general-purpose (DINOv2) and a domain-specific (RETFound) foundation model, across contrastive, masked-autoencoding, and self-distillation objectives, under frozen and fine-tuned protocols, and across labelled training sets from 100 to 32,250 examples. Which model performs best depends on how the encoder is used. When the encoder is frozen and labels are few -- the regime typical of longitudinal cohorts -- pre-trained representations reach clinically reasonable discrimination from a few hundred labelled samples, while models trained from scratch do not; this advantage fades under fine-tuning. Transfer is governed by the self-supervision objective rather than corpus scale or domain match, so that an encoder pre-trained on a modest cross-sectional cohort matches or exceeds a far larger in-domain foundation model. Together, these results offer a practical recipe for building progression models where longitudinal data is scarce: a frozen self-supervised encoder with a lightweight survival head.

</details>


### [21] [Pre-Trained Low-Rank Tensor Decomposition for Multi-Dimensional Image Recovery](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.12843)
*Bing-Zhang Fu,Zhi-Long Han,Ting-Zhu Huang,Xi-Le Zhao,Deyu Meng*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper proposes the first Pre-trained Low-Rank Tensor Decomposition (PLTD) framework that integrates pre-trained large vision models (DINOv3) into classical tensor decomposition for multi-dimensional image representation. The framework factorizes the target tensor into a latent tensor (combining fixed pre-trained features and learnable low-rank components) and a learnable transform, addressing limitations of traditional tensor decompositions that ignore common structures across images.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Traditional tensor decompositions learn instance-specific structures from scratch but neglect the common structure across different images, resulting in limited semantic modeling capability, high computational cost, and large numbers of learnable parameters. This work aims to address these challenges by incorporating pre-trained vision models into the tensor decomposition framework.

Method: PLTD factorizes the target tensor into: 1) A latent tensor consisting of two complementary terms - a fixed pre-trained latent tensor distilled from DINOv3 (capturing common structure) and a learnable low-rank latent tensor (capturing instance-specific structure); 2) A learnable transform that maps the latent tensor back to the original data domain. The framework was developed for multi-dimensional image recovery with theoretical justification.

Result: PLTD achieves an unprecedented balance among higher recovery fidelity, fewer learnable parameters, and smaller carbon footprint. Extensive experiments on multi-dimensional image recovery demonstrate superior performance compared with state-of-the-art methods.

Conclusion: The proposed PLTD framework successfully integrates pre-trained large vision models with tensor decomposition, effectively addressing the limitations of traditional approaches by capturing both common and instance-specific structures. This represents a novel paradigm in tensor decomposition research.

Abstract: Recently, tensor decompositions are prevalent for multi-dimensional image representation, which learn the instance-specific structure of each image from scratch. However, tensor decompositions neglect the common structure across different images, leading to limited semantic modeling capability, high computational cost, and a large number of learnable parameters. To address this challenge, we suggest the first pre-trained low-rank tensor decomposition (PLTD) framework, which organically integrates the pre-trained large vision model into the classical tensor decomposition framework. Beyond the shallow and untrained deep tensor decomposition, the suggested PLTD achieves an unprecedented balance among higher recovery fidelity, fewer learnable parameters, and smaller carbon footprint. Specifically, PLTD factorizes the target tensor into a latent tensor and a learnable transform that maps the latent tensor back to the original data domain. The latent tensor consists of two indispensable and complementary terms, i.e., a fixed pre-trained latent tensor and a learnable low-rank latent tensor. The fixed pre-trained latent tensor is distilled from a pre-trained large vision model (i.e., DINOv3) to capture the common structure of the target tensor, while the learnable low-rank latent tensor characterizes the instance-specific structure of the target tensor. To examine the potential of PLTD, we develop the corresponding multi-dimensional image recovery model and theoretically justify the advantages of this framework. Additionally, we discuss the connections between PLTD and classical tensor decomposition frameworks. Extensive experiments on multi-dimensional image recovery demonstrate that PLTD consistently achieves superior performance compared with state-of-the-art methods.

</details>


### [22] [MGAvatar: Mesh-Bound Gaussians for Head Avatar Geometry and Appearance Modeling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.12850)
*Lei Shi,Sen Peng,Zhiyang Deng,Zhonggui Chen,Xiaohu Guo,Baorong Yang,Xiao Dong*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: MGAvatar proposes a Gaussian-mesh hybrid representation for high-fidelity head avatar modeling, addressing limitations of parametric templates (like FLAME) that lack personalized priors and struggle with hair/clothing representation. The method uses two binding modes: vertex-bound Gaussians for progressive mesh deformation and face-bound Gaussians for appearance modeling, combined with view-conditioned neural color fields and Gaussian offset networks.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing Gaussian-based head avatars rely on parametric templates (e.g., FLAME) for initialization and deformation, but these templates lack personalized priors and cannot represent complex structures like hair and clothing. This limits the accuracy and expressiveness of head avatar models.

Method: The paper proposes MGAvatar, a Gaussian-mesh hybrid representation with two binding modes: (1) Vertex-bound Gaussians with constrained learnable parameters for progressive mesh deformation to represent complex head geometry, plus a pose-dependent offset module for non-rigid deformations; (2) Face-bound Gaussians for appearance modeling after geometry stabilization. Additional components include a view-conditioned neural color field to alleviate artifacts from independently optimized Gaussian colors, and a Gaussian offset network to predict offset maps in observation space for dynamic facial textures.

Result: Extensive experiments on multi-view and monocular videos show that MGAvatar outperforms existing methods in rendering quality, producing high-fidelity head avatars with rich texture details.

Conclusion: The Gaussian-mesh hybrid representation with dual binding modes effectively addresses the limitations of parametric templates, enabling more accurate and personalized head avatar modeling with better representation of complex geometries like hair and clothing.

Abstract: Accurate head modeling requires a stable yet expressive geometric representation. Existing Gaussian-based head avatars commonly rely on parametric templates (e.g., FLAME) for Gaussian initialization and deformation, but these templates lack personalized priors and struggle to represent structures such as hair and clothing. To address this issue, we propose MGAvatar, a Gaussian-mesh hybrid representation that jointly models geometry and appearance through two Gaussian-mesh binding modes. Specifically, we introduce vertex-bound Gaussians and constrain their learnable parameters, enabling progressive mesh deformation to represent complex head geometry, while a pose-dependent offset module accounts for non-rigid deformations. Once geometry is stabilized, MGAvatar switches to face-bound Gaussians for appearance modeling. To improve appearance consistency across novel poses and viewpoints, we introduce a view-conditioned neural color field that alleviates artifacts caused by independently optimized Gaussian colors. In addition, we design a Gaussian offset network to predict Gaussian offset maps in the observation space, providing greater flexibility for face-bound Gaussians to capture dynamic facial textures. Extensive experiments on multi-view and monocular videos show that MGAvatar outperforms existing methods in rendering quality, producing high-fidelity head avatars with rich texture details.

</details>


### [23] [3D CT-to-PET Translation via Latent Brownian Bridge Diffusion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.12860)
*Sarita Mourya,Francesco Di Feola,Pierangelo Veltri,Paolo Soda*

Main category: cs.CV
