Result: GLM 5.2 multimodal revision improved temporal agreement without reducing event recovery, performing competitively with clinician annotations. Other models showed smaller gains. UIDs primarily preserved event retention while source-row linkage supported temporal placement. Blinded human review upheld most GAVEL findings. Controlled adjudication favored multimodal over text-only for GLM 5.2 but not DeepSeek V3.2. Overall achieved 43% increased event recovery.

Conclusion: The UID-preserving framework with GAVEL evaluation system provides occurrence-level provenance for both timeline reconstruction and evaluation, achieving performance competitive with clinician annotations while significantly improving event recovery in clinical timeline extraction.

Abstract: Clinical timelines support treatment-window analysis and leakage-free modeling, but discharge summaries often obscure chronology and structured EHR tables describe only part of the patient course. We present a UID-preserving framework that links each narrative event occurrence to its source span and retains that identity through text-only estimation, structured-evidence retrieval, timestamped source-row grounding, and joint revision. We also present GAVEL, an LLM judge that compares two UID-aligned timelines against the narrative and structured record, to augment prior matching and temporal assessments. Across six open-weight models and 40 mixed-critical-care summaries, the GLM 5.2 multimodal revision, as compared to its text-only variant, improved temporal agreement without reducing event recovery and performed competitively with clinician annotations, while other model revisions showed smaller gains and lower overall performance. Ablations showed that UIDs primarily preserve event retention, whereas source-row linkage supports temporal placement. Blinded human review upheld most GAVEL findings, and controlled adjudication favored multimodal over text-only GLM 5.2 but did not for DeepSeek V3.2. In developing the UID and judge pipeline, we are able to demonstrate 43\% increased event recovery, a framework competitive with clinician annotations, and a system with occurrence-level provenance for both reconstruction and evaluation.

</details>


### [164] [Autonomous Research for Open-Ended Problems: A Case Study on Telecom Ticket Retrieval](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.13073)
*Junghyun Min,Huseyin Uzunalioglu,Mohamed Trabelsi*

Main category: cs.AI

TL;DR: This paper explores how autonomous LLM-based research systems can solve open-ended, industry-grade ML problems using telecom ticket retrieval as a case study. It finds that while autonomous research can achieve 90% of state-of-the-art performance in a fraction of the time and cost, it lacks human intuition and creativity, recommending human-AI collaboration.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Previous autonomous ML research frameworks have been limited to narrow search spaces (language modeling, biomedical benchmarks). The authors seek to adapt autonomous research for open-ended, industry-grade ML problems with flexibility in representation, architecture, and training data generation.

Method: Case study approach using telecom ticket retrieval as an open-ended task. Experiments used both commercial and open-source LLM agents with minimal human supervision to perform autonomous ML research.

Result: Autonomous research achieved 90% of state-of-the-art performance (0.34 vs 0.38 Recall@1) in 10 weeks compared to 10 months of human work, at a cost of up to $200 per Cursor campaign. Limitations include lack of human-like intuition and creativity, plus operational overhead.

Conclusion: Human researchers and autonomous research frameworks should work together for optimal results in ML research. Autonomous systems excel at narrow hyperparameter optimization but cannot fully replace human creativity and intuition in open-ended problems.

Abstract: Recent breakthroughs in LLM-based systems and their abilities in problem solving and coding have allowed progress in the AI for Science paradigm, potentially replacing human roles in machine learning (ML) research. However, while several frameworks of fully autonomous end-to-end ML research have been proposed, successful implementations of them are often limited to problems with narrow search spaces, like language modeling or biomedical ML benchmarks. In this paper, we explore how autonomous research can be adapted to solve open-ended, industry-grade ML problems, by considering a case study: telecom ticket retrieval, an open-ended task with degrees of freedom in representation, architecture, and training data generation. We discover that autonomous research for open-ended problems with commercial and open-source agents shows both promise and limitations: while autonomous research can excel in narrow hyperparameter optimization, it lacks human-like intuition and creativity and requires operational overhead. Even with minimal human supervision, autonomous research can reach $90\%$ of state-of-the-art performance (0.34 vs. 0.38 Recall@1) in a much shorter time period (10 weeks vs. 10 months of human work) at a modest cost (up to \$200 per Cursor campaign). Our empirical evidence recommends that human researchers and autonomous research frameworks work together for best results in ML research.

</details>


### [165] [Embodied-BenchForge: A Closed-Loop Agentic Workflow for Embodied Benchmark Construction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.13082)
*Baoyang Jiang,Fengchun Zhang,Leyuan Wang,Haotian Li,Yida Wang,Zhe Ji,Jinshan Lai,Xi Ren,Danyang Li,Zheng Yang,Jianwei Hu,Qiang Ma*

Main category: cs.AI

TL;DR: Embodied-BenchForge is an agentic framework that automates embodied benchmark construction by integrating forward artifact synthesis with backward verification and repair. It uses skill-orchestrated workflows and artifact dependency graphs to produce high-quality benchmarks while preventing defect propagation through requirement-guided verification.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing approaches for automated embodied benchmark construction are isolated, specialized to predefined environments, and lack artifact-specific verification. Multi-step construction produces dependent intermediate artifacts that propagate defects to final benchmarks without proper validation at each stage.

Method: The framework formulates construction as Closed-Loop Benchmark Synthesis with two key components: (1) Skill-Orchestrated Artifact Synthesis - composes typed and reusable skills into executable workflows; (2) Requirement-Guided Verification and Repair - applies artifact-specific contracts throughout construction and uses provenance to trigger local re-execution or upstream rollback when verification fails. An artifact dependency graph records intermediate outputs and their dependencies.

Result: Constructed six benchmarks covering diverse embodied scenarios in the Offline EQA Track and one interactive benchmark with 220 executable tasks in the Interactive Embodied Track. Evaluations of representative MLLMs and embodied agents show benchmarks distinguish model capabilities in both observation-based understanding and closed-loop execution. Quality assessment and ablations validate benchmark quality and the effectiveness of verification and repair. Repair and skill-reuse analyses demonstrate efficient localized recovery and cross-benchmark reusability.

Conclusion: Embodied-BenchForge successfully automates the construction of diverse, high-quality embodied benchmarks with built-in verification and repair mechanisms. The framework enables efficient localized recovery and cross-benchmark skill reuse, effectively distinguishing model capabilities in embodied AI tasks.

Abstract: Agentic systems offer a promising way to automate embodied benchmark construction, but existing approaches typically cover isolated stages or remain specialized to predefined environments and task families. More importantly, multi-step construction produces dependent intermediate artifacts that are often passed downstream without artifact-specific verification, allowing local defects to propagate into the final benchmark. We present Embodied-BenchForge, an agentic framework that transforms user-specified evaluation intents into complete embodied benchmark artifacts. It formulates construction as Closed-Loop Benchmark Synthesis, integrating forward artifact synthesis with backward verification and repair. Skill-Orchestrated Artifact Synthesis composes typed and reusable skills into executable workflows, while an artifact dependency graph records intermediate outputs and their dependencies. Requirement-Guided Verification and Repair applies artifact-specific contracts throughout construction and uses provenance to trigger local re-execution or upstream rollback when verification fails. Embodied-BenchForge constructs six benchmarks covering diverse embodied scenarios in the Offline EQA Track, together with one interactive benchmark containing 220 executable tasks in the Interactive Embodied Track. Evaluations of representative MLLMs and embodied agents show that the benchmarks distinguish model capabilities in both observation-based understanding and closed-loop execution. Quality assessment and ablations validate benchmark quality and the effectiveness of verification and repair, while repair and skill-reuse analyses demonstrate efficient localized recovery and cross-benchmark reusability.

</details>


### [166] [CMA-OT: Hierarchical Expert Supervision for Dance-to-Music Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.13118)
*Jinting Wang,Chenxing Li,Dong Yu,Li Liu*

Main category: cs.AI

TL;DR: This paper presents CMA-OT, a novel dance-to-music generation method that uses an external music expert to provide hierarchical supervision for learning better music representations. It addresses the semantic gap between sparse dance cues and dense music requirements through curriculum-guided multi-scale learning and scale-aware optimal transport alignment.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing D2M methods suffer from semantic mismatch between sparse dance cues (rhythm, style) and dense music requirements (structure, instrumentation, dynamics). Current approaches supervise only final audio output, resulting in poorly learned representations and low-quality generated music lacking musicality and structural coherence.

Method: The proposed CMA-OT framework has three key components: (1) External music expert providing hierarchical supervision for generator's latent features; (2) Curriculum-guided multi-scale learning strategy that progressively transfers musical knowledge from expert to generator, enabling stable and effective representation learning; (3) Scale-aware optimal transport alignment mechanism that models soft correspondences between hierarchical expert representations and generator features to handle semantic/structural variations and temporal mismatch.

Result: Extensive experiments on two datasets demonstrate that CMA-OT achieves state-of-the-art performance in rhythmic synchronization, perceptual quality, and overall music generation.

Conclusion: CMA-OT successfully bridges the semantic gap between dance cues and music composition requirements through hierarchical supervision from a music expert, curriculum-guided learning, and optimal transport alignment. The method significantly improves the quality and structural coherence of generated music aligned with dance videos.

Abstract: Dance-to-music (D2M) generation aims to synthesize music that is rhythmically and stylistically aligned with dance videos. A key challenge arises from the semantic mismatch between sparse dance cues, such as rhythm and style, and the dense information required for music composition, including structure, instrumentation, and expressive dynamics. Existing methods typically rely on these sparse cues and supervise only the final audio output, resulting in poorly learned music representations and generated music with limited musicality and structural coherence. To address these issues, we propose Curriculum-guided Multi-scale representation Alignment with scale-aware Optimal Transport (CMA-OT), a novel paradigm that leverages an external music expert to provide hierarchical supervision for the generator's latent features, bridging the semantic gap and enhancing representation learning. To effectively incorporate hierarchical supervision, we introduce a curriculum-guided multi-scale learning strategy that progressively transfers musical knowledge from the expert to the music generator, enabling stable and effective representation learning. Moreover, to accommodate the semantic and structural variations across different expert scales and achieve fine-grained alignment under temporal mismatch, we propose a scale-aware optimal transport alignment mechanism, which models soft correspondences between hierarchical expert representations and the generator's latent features. Extensive experiments on two datasets demonstrate that CMA-OT achieves state-of-the-art performance in rhythmic synchronization, perceptual quality, and overall music generation.

</details>


### [167] [A Hybrid LSTM-XGBoost Framework for Multi-Horizon Stock Return Prediction Across Diversified Equity Portfolios](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.13125)
*Seif ElDein Mostafa,Yahia Ahmed,Farah Datwish,Marwa Solayman*

Main category: cs.AI

TL;DR: This paper proposes a hybrid LSTM-XGBoost model for multi-horizon stock return prediction. The LSTM generates 64-dimensional temporal embeddings from 60-day sliding windows, which are concatenated with 14 technical indicators to form a 78-dimensional feature vector for XGBoost regression. The model is evaluated on 14 U.S. equities across 6 sectors at horizons of 30, 90, 252, and 365 days.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Accurate equity return prediction remains challenging due to the non-stationary, nonlinear, and low signal-to-noise ratio nature of financial time series. Existing single models (either purely deep learning or traditional ML) may not fully capture the complex temporal dynamics and cross-sectional patterns in stock returns.

Method: Hybrid two-stage architecture: (1) LSTM with two stacked layers (64 hidden units) processes 60-day sliding windows of 5 market features to produce 64-dimensional temporal embeddings; (2) These embeddings are concatenated with 14 hand-crafted technical indicators to form 78-dimensional hybrid feature vectors; (3) XGBoost regressor tuned via 3-fold cross-validation grid search. Training uses strict chronological splits and per-stock MinMaxScaling to prevent look-ahead bias. Evaluation across 4 prediction horizons (30, 90, 252, 365 trading days).

Result: Test RMSE of 0.0949 on 30-day horizon (roughly one-third that of standalone LSTM baseline). The hybrid model marginally matches or surpasses XGBoost-Only baseline across most stocks. Directional accuracy rises with horizon length, reaching 97.6% at 365 days, but this largely tracks the high base rate of positive long-horizon returns. A composite investment scoring framework is proposed for portfolio ranking.

Conclusion: The LSTM-XGBoost hybrid architecture effectively combines deep learning's sequential pattern recognition with gradient boosting's tabular data handling. The significant RMSE reduction versus standalone LSTM demonstrates the value of incorporating technical indicators. The directional accuracy analysis properly accounts for base rate bias, showing that short-horizon performance above the naive predictor is more informative.

Abstract: Accurate prediction of equity returns remains a major challenge in computational finance due to the non-stationary, nonlinear, and low signal-to-noise ratio nature of financial time series. This paper proposes a hybrid two-stage architecture that combines a long short-term memory (LSTM) network with an XGBoost gradient-boosted regressor for multi-horizon stock return prediction across a diversified panel of 14 U.S. equities spanning six industry sectors. The LSTM component, comprising two stacked layers with 64 hidden units, processes 60-day sliding windows of five sequential market features to produce 64-dimensional temporal embeddings that encode learned sequential market dynamics. These embeddings are concatenated with 14 hand-crafted technical indicators to form a 78-dimensional hybrid feature vector, which is subsequently passed to an XGBoost regressor tuned via 3-fold cross-validation grid search. The framework is trained on a multi-stock pooled corpus using strict chronological splits and per-stock MinMaxScaling to prevent look-ahead bias, and evaluated across four prediction horizons of 30, 90, 252, and 365 trading days. Experimental results demonstrate that the hybrid model achieves a test RMSE of 0.0949 on the 30-day horizon, roughly one-third that of the standalone LSTM baseline, while marginally matching or surpassing the XGBoost-Only baseline across the majority of stocks. Directional accuracy rises with horizon length, reaching 97.6% at 365 days; we show, however, that this largely tracks the high base rate of positive long-horizon returns in the sample, and we therefore benchmark directional accuracy against a naive always-positive predictor and treat the above-base-rate gap at short horizons as the more informative signal. A composite investment scoring framework derived from multi-horizon predictions is further proposed to support portfolio ranking and decision support.

</details>


### [168] [Rethinking Heterogeneous System Disaggregation for Subquadratic Attention](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.13134)
*Arya Tschand,Yaosheng Fu,Vikram Sharma Mailthody,Nicolai Oswald,Po-An Tsai,Ritchie Zhao,Oreste Villa,Vijay Janapa Reddi,Karu Sankaralingam*

Main category: cs.AI

TL;DR: SQD (SubQuadratic Disaggregation) is a novel inference serving scheme that splits decode operations by quadratic vs. subquadratic attention type rather than operator type, enabling significant throughput and energy efficiency gains on heterogeneous DRAM-based and SRAM-only systems for modern language models.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Frontier language models increasingly use subquadratic attention mechanisms to reduce memory and compute requirements, but existing systems make serving decisions based on dense attention-centric disaggregation. The authors aim to optimize inference by disaggregating based on the unique arithmetic intensity and memory footprint characteristics of subquadratic attention LLMs.

Method: SQD introduces fine-grained heterogeneous disaggregation that splits decode by attention type (quadratic vs. subquadratic) rather than operator type. For sparse attention LLMs, it separates top-k selection (which must index full KV cache) from top-k attention plus FFN (static memory footprint). For linear and sliding-window attention, it separates dense attention layers from subquadratic attention layers plus FFN. Experiments were conducted on an 8xB200 heterogeneous system proxy and an analytical model of Rubin plus LPX system.

Result: Average tokens/J improvements of 53% on GLM 5.2, 31% on Nemotron 3 Ultra, and 56% on Gemma 4 31B over strongest GPU-only baselines. In analytical models with fixed power budgets, 1.2x to 1.5x tighter achievable latencies and up to 3.6x higher throughput over the best attention-FFN disaggregation baseline.

Conclusion: The paper demonstrates that disaggregating inference around attention type (quadratic vs. subquadratic) rather than operator type enables significant performance gains on heterogeneous systems. It also provides architectural insights on chip and interconnect provisioning for next-generation heterogeneous systems serving subquadratic attention models.

Abstract: Frontier language models are more aggressively using subquadratic attention to reduce the memory footprint and compute requirements during inference while still delivering frontier accuracy. While existing systems make dense attention-centric disaggregated serving decisions, we show that disaggregating inference around the unique arithmetic intensity and memory footprint of subquadratic attention LLMs can achieve significant throughput and energy efficiency gains on emerging DRAM-based and SRAM-only heterogeneous systems.
  We introduce SQD (SubQuadratic Disaggregation), a fine-grained heterogeneous disaggregation scheme that splits decode by quadratic and subquadratic attention rather than by operator type, and that applies across subquadratic attention variants. For sparse attention LLMs, we disaggregate decode into top-k selection, which must index through the full KV, and top-k attention plus FFN, which have static memory footprints. For linear and sliding-window attention LLMs, we disaggregate decode into dense attention layers and subquadratic attention layers plus FFN. In an adjusted 8xB200 heterogeneous system proxy, we observe average tokens/J improvements of 53% on GLM 5.2, 31% on Nemotron 3 Ultra, and 56% on Gemma 4 31B over the strongest GPU-only baselines. In an analytical model of a Rubin plus LPX system with fixed power budgets, we observe 1.2x to 1.5x tighter achievable latencies and up to 3.6x higher throughput over the best baseline of attention-FFN disaggregation. Our experiments also reveal architectural insights on chip and interconnect provisioning for next-generation heterogeneous systems serving subquadratic attention.

</details>


### [169] [Evidence-Aligned Local Composition of Discrete Experts for Sequence Restoration](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.05801)
*Mohammad Panahazari,Usman A. Khan,Shuchin Aeron*

Main category: cs.AI

TL;DR: This paper introduces evidence-aligned local composition, a method for restoring corrupted documents by inferring position-wise weighting over frozen domain experts. The method estimates evidence from experts' denoising losses and smooths weights across positions, allowing it to recover mixtures when needed or concentrate on a single expert when appropriate.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: When documents containing mixed domains (prose, code, configuration) are corrupted and only frozen domain experts are available, restoration requires deciding both what is missing and which expert to trust at each position - without region labels or a trained router at test time.

Method: Evidence-aligned local composition infers soft, position-wise weighting over experts from the marginal evidence of the corrupted observation under a given corruption model. The evidence is estimated from the experts' own denoising losses and smoothed across positions.

Result: The inferred weights track true regions at 0.85 field accuracy on naturally mixed scientific documents, and at 0.98 on constructed mixtures whose regions are lexically disjoint. Restoration improves over single global weight when experts are genuinely distinct and reduces to it when experts converge.

Conclusion: The method successfully handles both mixed and concentrated expert compositions, adaptively tracking a measure of expert separation. It provides a principled approach to document restoration without requiring region labels or trained routers, working effectively across categorical simulators, byte-level experts, and flow-matching models.

Abstract: A document modeled as a discrete sequence of tokens can be thought of as being generated from a composition of texts from different domains; a README file, for example, moves between prose, code, and configuration. When such a document is corrupted and only frozen domain experts are available, restoring it requires deciding both what is missing and which expert to trust at each position, at test time and without region labels or a trained router. We introduce evidence-aligned local composition, which infers a soft, position-wise weighting over the experts from the marginal evidence of the corrupted observation under a given corruption model, estimating the evidence from the experts' own denoising losses and smoothing the weights across positions. Because the weighting is soft, it recovers a mixture when the true composition is mixed and concentrates on one expert when that suffices. Across a categorical simulator, byte-level experts, and experts fine-tuned from a $1.3$B discrete flow-matching model, the inferred weights track the true regions at $0.85$ field accuracy on naturally mixed scientific documents, and at $0.98$ on constructed mixtures whose regions are lexically disjoint. Restoration improves over a single global weight when the experts are genuinely distinct and reduces to it when they converge, tracking a measure of expert separation.

</details>


### [170] [Robust Prototypical Networks for Few-Shot Sensor Fault Diagnosis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.12287)
*Mohammed Ayalew Belay,Amirshayan Haghipour,Pierluigi Salvo Rossi*

Main category: cs.AI

TL;DR: This paper proposes Multi-Episode Prototypical Networks (MEPN) for industrial fault diagnosis in few-shot learning scenarios. The method addresses prototype instability in very-low-shot regimes by aggregating prototypes from multiple disjoint support episodes, reducing variance without changing the encoder architecture.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Industrial fault diagnosis often operates with only a handful of labeled fault examples, making few-shot learning attractive for sensor monitoring. Standard prototypical networks suffer from unstable class prototypes in the very-low-shot regime because each decision relies on a small support set.

Method: MEPN aggregates prototypes from multiple disjoint support episodes and uses their mean as the final class representative. This reduces prototype variance without changing the encoder architecture. The evaluation uses five-way fault classification on the DeFACTO sensor dataset with synthetic bias, drift, spike, and noise faults injected into real industrial measurements.

Result: Over 100 independent runs, MEPN reaches 83.2% in the per-episode one-shot setting (K=1 shot, aggregated over N_agg=10 support episodes), substantially above single-episode baselines. Under an equal 10-sample support budget, MEPN and ProtoNet at K=10 are statistically indistinguishable, confirming prototype accumulation as the mechanism rather than superior fixed-budget learning.

Conclusion: Prototype aggregation from multiple episodes effectively reduces variance in very-low-shot fault diagnosis. The mechanism is confirmed to be prototype accumulation rather than superior fixed-budget learning, as MEPN performs comparably to ProtoNet with K=10 when given equal 10-sample support budget.

Abstract: Industrial fault diagnosis often operates with only a handful of labeled fault examples, making few-shot learning attractive for sensor monitoring. Standard prototypical networks are simple and effective; however, their class prototypes may become unstable in the very-low-shot regime because each decision relies on a small support set. We propose \emph{Multi-Episode Prototypical Networks} (MEPN), which aggregate prototypes from multiple disjoint support episodes and use their mean as the final class representative, reducing prototype variance without changing the encoder architecture. We evaluate MEPN on the DeFACTO sensor dataset using five-way fault classification with synthetic bias, drift, spike, and noise faults injected into real industrial measurements. Over 100 independent runs, MEPN reaches \textbf{\SensorOneShotGcpn\%} in the per-episode one-shot setting ($K\!=\!1$ shot, aggregated over $N_{\text{agg}}\!=\!10$ support episodes), substantially above single-episode baselines. Under an equal 10-sample support budget, MEPN and ProtoNet at $K\!=\!10$ are statistically indistinguishable, confirming prototype accumulation as the mechanism rather than superior fixed-budget learning.

</details>


### [171] [Affective Agent: On-Device Personalized Intervention Reasoning for Wearable Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.12322)
*Reina Mun,Zishen Wan,Vijay Janapa Reddi*

Main category: cs.AI

TL;DR: Affective Agent is a three-layer architecture enabling personalized intervention reasoning on wearable hardware by combining a compact language model with physiological signals, context, and user history, without cloud dependency or per-user retraining.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Affective computing has advanced wearable state inference, but on-device reasoning about whether, when, and how to intervene remains challenging due to hardware constraints and the need for personalization.

Method: The architecture uses three interacting layers (perception, personalization, and reasoning) with a sub-billion-parameter language model. It adapts to individual users through host-managed structured memory evolution rather than per-user weight updates, enabling two-pass structured reasoning for intervention decisions.

Result: Memory-driven personalization and two-pass structured reasoning improve intervention decisions within synthetic evaluation scenarios spanning physiological variation, context, signal quality, and intervention history.

Conclusion: This work demonstrates a viable path from wearable state inference toward closed-loop, personalized intervention on wearable-class hardware by moving the decision layer on-device.

Abstract: Affective computing has advanced wearable state inference, but on-device reasoning about whether, when, and how to intervene remains challenging. We present Affective Agent, a three-layer reference architecture for personalized intervention reasoning under uncertainty on wearable-class hardware. It combines a compact sub-billion-parameter language model with physiological evidence, context, and user history to decide whether, when, and how to intervene, without cloud dependency or per-user retraining. The architecture is organized into three interacting layers (perception, personalization, and reasoning), adapting to individual users through host-managed structured memory evolution rather than per-user weight updates. We instantiate Affective Agent in indoor environmental quality control and evaluate it on held-out, simulator-generated longitudinal scenarios spanning physiological variation, context, signal quality, and intervention history. Results show that memory-driven personalization and two-pass structured reasoning improve intervention decisions within this synthetic evaluation. By moving the decision layer on-device, this work demonstrates a path from wearable state inference toward closed-loop, personalized intervention on wearable-class hardware.

</details>
